== DTP OCAH Open-Source Testbench Architecture

=== Overview

[width="100%",cols="50%,50%",options="header",]
|===
|Field |Value
|DUT |`dtp` (`hw/dtp/rtl/dtp.sv`)

|Repository |`tt-oca`

|Project |OCAH

|Framework |PyUVM on cocotb

|Primary simulator |Verilator

|Commercial simulator use |Coverage and CSR paths that Verilator cannot
elaborate today

|Reference TB |`dv/dtp/` working DTP environment and docs
|===

The OCAH open-source DTP testbench follows the canonical
`hw/sys/++<++system++>++/dv/` layout under `hw/sys/dtp/dv/`. It ports
the working reference plan from `dv/dtp/` into a public PyUVM
environment using the unified OCAH BFM packages for standard interfaces.

=== VIP Selection Policy

[arabic]
. Import unified OCAH BFM wrappers from `hw/common/dv/vip` for standard
protocol access.
. Wrap those BFMs in PyUVM agents so tests remain sequence-based and
reusable.
. Use OCAH-local BFMs/models for custom OCH protocols and standards
without a shared wrapper yet.
. Keep every BFM behind the same transaction-level agent boundary so
backend changes do not affect sequences or scoreboards.

[width="100%",cols="29%,26%,33%,12%",options="header",]
|===
|DTP Interface |Preferred VIP |Modeling Approach |Notes
|Primary JTAG TAP |`ocah++_++jtag++_++vip` `OcahJtagTap` |Unified OCAH
BFM |Active JTAG transaction driver and TAP helper

|SMC fabric AXI4 manager |`ocah++_++axi++_++vip` `OcahAxiRam` |Unified
OCAH BFM |AXI memory responder with scoreboard backdoor access

|SMC/SEP OTP AXI-Lite managers |`ocah++_++axi++_++vip` AXI-Lite wrapper
|Unified OCAH BFM |AXI-Lite responder model for OTP bridge scenarios

|XTRIG AXI-Lite CSR subordinate |`ocah++_++axi++_++vip`
`OcahAxiLiteMaster` |Unified OCAH BFM |CSR access model for XTRIG
scenarios

|BSR/STAP scan loopbacks |Minimal OCAH-local scan model |OCAH-local
simple model |Compact scan loopback and observable model

|iJTAG SIB networks |OCAH-local IEEE 1687 model |OCAH-local model |IEEE
1687 scan-network transaction model

|CTP/CTM cross-trigger protocols |OCAH-local BFMs |OCAH-local BFM
|CTP/CTM protocol BFMs; debug TDR clock-stop requests may be driven
directly in the TB
|===

=== Testbench Hierarchy

[source,text]
----
dtp_<scenario>_test (@pyuvm.test)
  -> dtp_base_test
       build_phase:
         DtpEnvCfg -> ConfigDB
         DtpEnv
       run_phase:
         clock/reset bring-up
         cfg.reset_done event
         start_seq(test sequence)

DtpEnv
  -> DtpJtagAgent
       sequencer
       DtpJtagDriver -> ocah_jtag_vip OcahJtagTap
       analysis port -> completed DtpJtagItem stream
  -> DtpAxiAgent
       ocah_axi_vip OcahAxiRam
       backdoor memory access for scoreboard checks
  -> DtpScoreboard
       IDCODE checks
       JTAG2AXI write/read data integrity checks
----

----

=== HDL Top

`tb/tb++_++top.sv` instantiates `dtp` as `dtp++_++uvm++_++top` and
provides signal adapters between packed RTL structs and cocotb-friendly
scalar/vector ports.

[width="100%",cols="45%,55%",options="header",]
|===
|DUT Interface |`tb++_++top` Handling
|JTAG TAP |Exposes `jtag++_++tck`, `jtag++_++tms`, `jtag++_++trst`,
`jtag++_++tdi`, `jtag++_++tdo`, `jtag++_++tdo++_++oen` for `OcahJtagTap`

|SMC fabric AXI4 |Flattens `axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_*++` structs into
`m++_++axi++_*++` for `OcahAxiRam.from++_++prefix(dut, "m++_++axi")`

|Lifecycle |Drives enable-polarity `feat++_++ctrl++_++i` from TB; base
bring-up uses the all-one/full-debug setting

|BSR/STAP/iJTAG |Looped back or tied idle for scenarios that do not
actively use these paths

|SMC/SEP OTP AXI-Lite |Tied idle until OCAH AXI-Lite responder agents
are added

|DEBUG++_++CONTROL and IC++_++RESET |Flattens boot-stall, clock-stop,
and IC++_++RESET struct outputs into cocotb-visible scalar signals

|XTRIG CSR/CTP/CTM |CSR blocks tied idle or stubbed for Verilator;
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req` is directly driven for
DEBUG++_++CONTROL clock-stop tests

|Clocks/resets |cocotb drives `clk++_++i`, `rst++_++n++_++i`, and
`pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni`; JTAG driver owns TCK/TRST sequencing
|===

=== Environment Components

==== `DtpJtagAgent`

The active JTAG agent wraps `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip.OcahJtagTap`. It
accepts `DtpJtagItem` transactions for TAP reset, raw TMS steps, IR/DR
scans, IDCODE reads, generic named-TDR reads/writes, and JTAG2AXI single
operations. The driver initializes the BFM, resets the TAP, and
broadcasts completed items on its analysis port.

For writable TDR reads, the transaction can carry a caller-selected DR
`shift++_++value`. The default remains zero to expose unintended side
effects, while debug TDR sequences pass preserve values when the
scenario must hold TMP++_++STATUS, IC++_++RESET, or DEBUG++_++CONTROL
fields across the read.

==== `DtpAxiAgent`

The AXI agent wraps `ocah++_++axi++_++vip.OcahAxiRam` on the SMC fabric
debug manager interface. It provides a memory responder for AXI
writes/reads and a backdoor view used by the scoreboard.

==== `DtpScoreboard`

The scoreboard subscribes to completed JTAG items. It verifies IDCODE
bit 0, checks JTAG2AXI write data against the OCAH AXI RAM backdoor, and
checks read data against preloaded memory contents. `check++_++phase`
requires zero errors.

==== Sequence Library

The DTP sequence library uses a hierarchical helper model. The generic
base sequence owns only cross-cutting sequence infrastructure. Feature
base sequences then collect helpers for one verification domain, such as
Basic JTAG, debug TDR, or JTAG2AXI.

Directed tests should inherit from the narrowest feature base that
matches the scenario. This keeps each concrete test readable and makes
helper ownership obvious. Random and stress tests should prefer
composition: instantiate small command-library sequence helpers for each
domain and keep the random orchestration in the top-level stress
sequence.

Conceptually:

[width="100%",cols="54%,46%",options="header",]
|===
|Layer |Role
|Generic base sequence |Common sequence infrastructure shared by all
scenarios

|Feature base sequence |Domain-specific directed-test helpers

|Command-library sequence |Small reusable operations for random/stress
composition

|Concrete test sequence |One readable scenario flow for one
`@pyuvm.test` wrapper
|===

New tests should keep the one-sequence plus one-`@pyuvm.test` pattern:
`seq++_++lib/++<++test++_++name++>_++seq.py` and
`tests/++<++test++_++name++>++.py`. Test wrappers and test classes end
in `++_++test`; concrete test sequence files/classes end in
`++_++test++_++seq`; reusable helper sequence/library files/classes also
end in `++_++seq`.

=== Clock and Reset Strategy

[cols=",,",options="header",]
|===
|Clock / Reset |Source |Notes
|`clk++_++i` |cocotb clock in base test |System/AXI/XTRIG domain
|JTAG TCK |`OcahJtagTap` |TAP clock domain
|`rst++_++n++_++i` |base test |Active-low system reset
|`pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni` |base test |Power-on reset for JTAG logic
|TRST |`OcahJtagTap` |TAP reset
|===

JTAG2AXI sequences poll response status instead of sleeping for a fixed
number of cycles. This keeps CDC and AXI latency handling
protocol-based.

=== Stimulus Strategy

The open-source stimulus strategy is mostly directed and deterministic,
with seeded randomization in the debug TDR and Basic JTAG pattern sweeps
where it adds coverage value:

[width="100%",cols="38%,62%",options="header",]
|===
|Area |Strategy
|Smoke |Verify TAP FSM traversal and IDCODE readout through focused JTAG
tests

|Basic JTAG |Verify primary TAP instruction decode, bypass variants,
reset behavior, and compact BSR/iJTAG loopback smoke behavior

|Debug TDR |Verify TMP++_++STATUS, IC++_++RESET, DEBUG++_++CONTROL,
JTAG++_++CAPS, and JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS with decoded logs, deterministic
loops, seeded random patterns, and read-only sweeps

|JTAG2AXI write |Shift a single-op write request, poll completion, check
OCAH AXI RAM memory

|JTAG2AXI read |Backdoor-preload OCAH AXI RAM, shift a single-op read
request, poll completion, compare data
|===

Additional stimulus categories are derived from the reference
`dv/dtp/doc/DTP++_++VPLAN.md`: OTP bridge operations, STAP/3DCR, iJTAG,
XTRIG CSR, CTP, and CTM.

=== Checking Strategy

[width="100%",cols="34%,30%,36%",options="header",]
|===
|Check Type |Component |Description
|TAP data |`DtpJtagDriver`, `DtpScoreboard` |IDCODE and TDR returned
data

|Debug TDR observables |`tb++_++top.sv`, `DtpJtagDriver` samples
|Boot-stall, clock-stop, IC++_++RESET, and decoded-instruction
observables

|AXI data integrity |`DtpScoreboard` |JTAG2AXI writes/reads against OCAH
AXI RAM

|Protocol completion |`DtpJtagDriver` |JTAG2AXI status polling until
non-busy

|Test completion |PyUVM phases |Objections drop only after sequences
finish

|Regression parsing |Global DV parser |Requires positive cocotb result
evidence
|===

=== Coverage Strategy

Verilator does not support SV covergroups. The OCAH open-source path
should collect Python-side functional observations in the PyUVM
environment and rely on commercial simulators for SV covergroups,
assertion coverage, and full code coverage. See `DTP++_++FCOV.md`.

=== Verilator Stubs

`tb/verilator++_++stubs/` contains:

[width="100%",cols="40%,60%",options="header",]
|===
|Stub |Purpose
|`prim++_++sync2.sv` |Maps the public synchronizer wrapper to legacy
port naming

|`cross++_++trigger++_++port++_++reg.sv` |Replaces generated CTP CSR
block for Verilator

|`cross++_++trigger++_++matrix++_++reg.sv` |Replaces generated CTM CSR
block for Verilator
|===

The CSR stubs complete no real CSR behavior and drive hardware outputs
inactive. Functional XTRIG CSR, CTP, and CTM tests must remove these
stubs and use a commercial simulator, or replace the generated blocks
with a Verilator-safe CSR implementation.

=== Build and Run

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_sanity_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag_idcode_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items basic_jtag
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items debug_tdr
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_wr_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_rd_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items functional
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items all --dry-run
----

The `smoke` group remains a fast two-test TAP/IDCODE gate. The
`basic++_++jtag` group runs all 19 Smoke and Basic JTAG scenarios. The
`debug++_++tdr` group runs 12 TMP++_++STATUS, IC++_++RESET,
DEBUG++_++CONTROL, and CAPS scenarios. The `functional` group runs the
legacy four public DTP smoke/JTAG2AXI tests, while `all` collects the
included public DTP testlists.

== DTP OCAH Open-Source Verification Plan

=== Overview

[width="100%",cols="50%,50%",options="header",]
|===
|Field |Value
|Design Under Test |`dtp`

|Repository |`tt-oca`

|Project |OCAH

|Framework |PyUVM on cocotb

|Primary simulator |Verilator

|Reference SPEC |`DTP++_++SPEC.md`

|Reference CSR |`DTP++_++CSR.md`

|Internal reference plan |`dv/dtp/doc/DTP++_++VPLAN.md` (internal full
plan; not shipped in the public OCAH package)

|Reference logical tests |72

|Open-source scenario-expanded test cases |147
|===

This OCAH open-source VPLAN is the public, standalone DTP verification
plan. It is derived from the internal full DTP reference plan and
includes all of its tests, expanding multi-scenario CocoTB modules into
separate, named test cases. The open-source implementation uses unified
OCAH BFM wrappers for standard protocols and OCAH-local DTP BFMs for
DTP-specific protocols.

This plan is synced to the verified closed-source DTP reference VPLAN as
of 2026-06-15. The closed-source plan remains the 72 logical-test source
of truth; this OSS document expands those logical tests into 147 public
scenario-level entries.

=== Verification Strategy

The DTP open-source environment is a PyUVM-on-cocotb testbench run on
Verilator. Each test builds `dtp++_++uvm++_++top`, brings up `clk++_++i`
and the `pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni` / `rst++_++n++_++i` resets, resets
the primary TAP, and drives stimulus through the OCAH BFMs:

[arabic]
. *Stimulus*: standard protocols use the unified OCAH VIP wrappers —
`ocah++_++jtag++_++vip` (`OcahJtagTap`) for the JTAG TAP, and
`ocah++_++axi++_++vip` (`OcahAxiRam`, AXI-Lite wrappers,
`OcahAxiLiteMaster`) for the SMC fabric / OTP debug and XTRIG CSR buses.
DTP-specific protocols (boundary-scan / STAP loopbacks, IEEE 1687 iJTAG
SIB networks, OCH CTP/CTM) use OCAH-local models in `env/` (see
Open-Source BFM Policy).
. *Checking*: a scoreboard / reference model compares observed TAP scan
data, AXI transactions, and cross-trigger routing against expected
behavior. Unless a procedure states otherwise, each test ends by
requiring zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.
. *Lifecycle*: lifecycle controls are held in the full-debug-enabled
state by default; security-gating scenarios drive `feat++_++ctrl++_++i`
explicitly.
. *Coverage*: SystemVerilog covergroups are not supported by Verilator,
so functional coverage is collected Python-side (see `DTP++_++FCOV.md`)
or on a commercial simulator.
. *Scenario expansion*: multi-scenario reference modules are expanded
into separate, named open-source test cases (see Naming Rules) so each
scenario is independently selectable in the public testlists.

=== Naming Rules

[width="100%",cols="32%,68%",options="header",]
|===
|Rule |Requirement
|Single-scenario module |Use the module name, for example
`dtp++_++jtag++_++idcode++_++test`

|Multi-scenario module |Use `dtp++_<++feature++>_<++scenario++>_++test`,
for example `dtp++_++ctm++_++p2p++_++cla++_++to++_++ctp++_++test`

|Scenario names |Use descriptive lower-snake-case names that encode
operation, mode, direction, or coverage target

|Legacy non-DTP names |Normalize to `dtp++_*++`, for example the legacy
SMC AXI error single-write scenario becomes
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++error++_++single++_++write++_++test`

|Open-source files |Add one PyUVM test class in `tests/` and one
sequence in `seq++_++lib/` per scenario-expanded test case
|===

=== Open-Source BFM Policy

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Interface / Protocol |Open-Source Replacement
|Primary JTAG TAP |`ocah++_++jtag++_++vip` `OcahJtagTap`
|AXI4 SMC fabric debug |`ocah++_++axi++_++vip` `OcahAxiRam`
|AXI4-Lite SMC/SEP OTP debug |`ocah++_++axi++_++vip` AXI-Lite wrappers
|AXI4-Lite XTRIG CSR |`ocah++_++axi++_++vip` `OcahAxiLiteMaster`
|Boundary scan / STAP loopbacks |OCAH-local scan responder in `env/`
|IEEE 1687 iJTAG SIB networks |OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model in `env/`
|OCH CTP protocol |OCAH-local CTP BFM in `env/`
|OCH CTM routing |OCAH-local CTM BFM and monitors in `env/`
|===

=== Test Procedures

Unless a test procedure states otherwise, each test starts from the
common DTP bring-up sequence: build `dtp++_++uvm++_++top`, start
`clk++_++i`, assert and release
`pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni`/`rst++_++n++_++i`, reset the primary TAP,
use the configured OCAH BFMs, and keep lifecycle controls in the
full-debug enabled state. Each procedure ends by checking
scoreboard/reference-model errors are zero.

==== 0. Smoke and Basic JTAG

===== 0.1 `dtp++_++sanity++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++sanity++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`

*Objective*: TAP FSM coverage: reset, DR/IR/pause path traversal,
randomized state navigation, all IEEE 1149.1 states visited (IDCODE
moved to `dtp++_++jtag++_++idcode++_++test`)

*Test Coverage*:

* Core TMS/TCK/TDI/TDO datapath driven through a complete IR/DR scan
sequence
* TAP FSM traversal of all targeted IEEE 1149.1 states: TLR, RTI,
Select-DR/IR, Capture, Shift, Pause, Update
* Randomized state navigation so every legal transition edge is
exercised at least once
* BYPASS load (IR=6 bits, 0x00) produces a single-cycle TDI-to-TDO
latency during Shift-DR
* `tap++_++fsm++_++cg` state visitation and `sanity++_++cg`
basic-operational-state reached

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Reset the primary TAP to Test-Logic-Reset, then step to Run-Test/Idle.
. Through `OcahJtagTap`, sequence TLR → RTI → Select-DR → Capture-DR →
Shift-DR → Update-DR → RTI, sampling `jtag++_++ptap++_++state++_++o`
after each TCK.
. Drive an IR scan RTI → Select-DR → Select-IR → Capture-IR → Shift-IR
(6 bits, BYPASS 0x00) → Update-IR → RTI.
. With BYPASS active, run a 1-bit DR scan and confirm the single-TCK
TDI-to-TDO delay.
. Drive randomized raw-TMS paths through DR/IR/pause/update legs until
every IEEE 1149.1 state and legal transition is observed.
. Return to TLR via at least five TMS=1 cycles.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sanity++_++fsm++_++visit++_++chk` |Every targeted IEEE 1149.1 state
and legal transition is observed on `jtag++_++ptap++_++state++_++o`

|`sanity++_++bypass++_++latency++_++chk` |BYPASS (IR=0x00) yields
exactly a 1-TCK TDI-to-TDO delay in Shift-DR

|`sanity++_++scan++_++path++_++chk` |IR/DR scan sequence completes and
the TAP returns to Run-Test/Idle cleanly
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The TAP sequences correctly through all targeted states
with every IEEE 1149.1 state visited and BYPASS showing 1-bit latency,
with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 0.2 `dtp++_++jtag++_++bypass++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++bypass++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`

*Objective*: BYPASS `0x00` and `0x3F`

*Test Coverage*:

* Both BYPASS encodings (IR=0x00 and IR=0x3F) present a 1-bit DR shift
register between TDI and TDO
* TDO equals TDI delayed by exactly one TCK cycle during Shift-DR for
each encoding
* Capture-DR loads 0 into the bypass register per IEEE 1149.1
* `bypass++_++cg` (both BYPASS opcodes exercised) and
`ir++_++opcode++_++cg` 0x00 / 0x3F bins

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load IR = 0x00 (BYPASS) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Shift a known 64-bit pattern (alternating 0/1) through DR and confirm
TDO == TDI delayed by exactly 1 bit.
. Verify Capture-DR loaded 0 into the bypass register (leading captured
bit is 0).
. Load IR = 0x3F (BYPASS all-ones) and repeat the same 64-bit pattern
shift.
. Confirm identical 1-bit-delay behavior and DR length of 1 for both
encodings.
. Return to Run-Test/Idle.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`bypass++_++dr++_++len++_++chk` |DR chain length is exactly 1 bit for
both IR=0x00 and IR=0x3F

|`bypass++_++latency++_++chk` |TDO equals TDI delayed by one TCK cycle
during Shift-DR

|`bypass++_++capture++_++chk` |Capture-DR loads 0 into the bypass
register
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Both BYPASS encodings produce a 1-bit register with
one-cycle TDI-to-TDO delay and a 0 capture value, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 0.3 `dtp++_++jtag++_++undef++_++instr++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++undef++_++instr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`

*Objective*: Undefined instructions select BYPASS

*Test Coverage*:

* All undefined/reserved IR encodings (e.g. range 0x10-0x17 and other
unused opcodes) default to BYPASS
* Each reserved opcode produces 1-bit bypass behavior (TDI -++>++ 1-bit
delay -++>++ TDO)
* No spurious TDR selection, no output changes, and no error conditions
triggered
* `undef++_++instr++_++cg` (each tested reserved opcode) and
`bypass++_++fallback++_++cg` (undefined -++>++ BYPASS)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each reserved encoding in 0x10-0x17 and any other unused opcode:
[loweralpha]
.. Load the reserved IR value through `OcahJtagTap`.
.. Run a DR scan with a known pattern and confirm 1-bit bypass behavior
(TDI delayed one TCK to TDO).
. While iterating, confirm no other TDR is selected and no
boundary/output side effects occur.
. Verify no error/fault condition is raised for any reserved encoding.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`undef++_++bypass++_++fallback++_++chk` |Each reserved IR opcode
selects the 1-bit BYPASS path

|`undef++_++no++_++tdr++_++select++_++chk` |No functional TDR is
selected and no outputs change for any reserved opcode

|`undef++_++no++_++error++_++chk` |No error/fault condition is raised by
any undefined encoding
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every tested reserved/undefined IR encoding falls back
to 1-bit BYPASS with no spurious TDR selection, output change, or error,
with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 0.4 `dtp++_++jtag++_++idcode++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++idcode++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`

*Objective*: IDCODE field verification

*Test Coverage*:

* 32-bit IDCODE TDR auto-loaded on power-up and after Test-Logic-Reset
* Field decode: `SI++_++REV++[++31:28++]++`,
`PART++_++NUM++[++27:12++]++`, `MFR++_++ID++[++11:1++]++`,
`MARKER++[++0++]++=1`
* IDCODE equals
`++{++JTAG++_++IDCODE++_++SI++_++REV, JTAG++_++IDCODE++_++PART++_++NUM, JTAG++_++IDCODE++_++MFR++_++ID, 1'b1}`
* IDCODE consistency across power-up, TLR, and explicit IDCODE
instruction load
* `idcode++_++cg`: IDCODE read after TLR and after explicit IR load

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Reset the primary TAP to Test-Logic-Reset so IDCODE auto-loads.
. Without loading any IR, perform a 32-bit DR scan through `OcahJtagTap`
and capture TDO.
. Decode and check fields `SI++_++REV++[++31:28++]++`,
`PART++_++NUM++[++27:12++]++`, `MFR++_++ID++[++11:1++]++`, and
`MARKER++[++0++]++ == 1`.
. Explicitly load IDCODE (IR=0x01); repeat the 32-bit DR scan.
. Force TLR again and re-read; require an identical value on every read.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`idcode++_++value++_++chk` |Captured IDCODE equals
`++{++SI++_++REV, PART++_++NUM, MFR++_++ID, 1'b1}` from compile-time
parameters

|`idcode++_++marker++_++chk` |MARKER bit (bit 0) is always 1

|`idcode++_++stable++_++chk` |IDCODE identical across power-up, explicit
IR load, and post-TLR reads
|===

*Pass Criteria*: IDCODE matches the parameter-defined value with the
marker bit set and is stable across all three read paths, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 0.5 `dtp++_++jtag++_++runbist++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++runbist++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG/DFT model

*Objective*: RUNBIST and DFT gating

*Test Coverage*:

* RUNBIST (IR=0x02) accepted by the TAP and not redirected to BYPASS,
with BIST executing during Run-Test/Idle and a result captured via DR
* RUNBIST drives the non-secure DFT iJTAG instrument, gated by
`feat++_++ctrl++_++i` via `dft++_++nonsecure++_++security++_++disable`
* `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug` does NOT suppress the RUNBIST
instruction itself; it only gates the non-secure DFT SIB
* With
`dft++_++nonsecure++_++security++_++disable = !soc++_++debug ++||++ !ap++_++debug`
asserted, the DFT SIB ignores open requests and the DFT instrument
register cannot be updated
* After restoring required enables,
`dft++_++nonsecure++_++security++_++disable` deasserts, the DFT SIB is
reachable again, and writes succeed
* `runbist++_++cg` (RUNBIST executed) and `runbist++_++security++_++cg`
(ungated / DFT SIB gated / DFT SIB restored)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load RUNBIST (IR=0x02) through `OcahJtagTap` and enter Run-Test/Idle
to execute BIST.
. Hold RTI for the required cycles, then Capture-DR/Shift-DR to read the
BIST pass/fail result.
. Security-gating scenario:
[loweralpha]
.. Drive required enable `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug = 0`
(asserts `dft++_++nonsecure++_++security++_++disable`).
.. Load RUNBIST and enter RTI; confirm the TAP accepts the instruction
regardless of `feat++_++ctrl++_++i`.
.. Seed the OCAH-local DFT model with a sentinel value, attempt to
overwrite the DFT instrument register through the iJTAG path, and
confirm the model register is unchanged (SIB forced closed).
. Restore `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug = 1`; repeat the iJTAG DFT
write and confirm the model register now updates (SIB accessible again).

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`runbist++_++accept++_++chk` |RUNBIST is accepted by the TAP (not
redirected to BYPASS) and BIST runs during RTI

|`runbist++_++instr++_++ungated++_++chk`
|`feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug` does not suppress the RUNBIST
instruction itself

|`dft++_++sib++_++gated++_++chk` |With
`dft++_++nonsecure++_++security++_++disable` asserted, the DFT
instrument register cannot be written

|`dft++_++sib++_++restored++_++chk` |After restoring required enables,
the DFT SIB is reachable and the write succeeds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: RUNBIST is always accepted and executes BIST while the
non-secure DFT SIB is open only when
`dft++_++nonsecure++_++security++_++disable` is deasserted, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 0.6 `dtp++_++jtag++_++sample++_++preload++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++sample++_++preload++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, OCAH-local BSR model

*Objective*: SAMPLE/PRELOAD

*Test Coverage*:

* SAMPLE/PRELOAD (IR=0x03) captures live boundary pin state without
disturbing functional outputs
* Captured BSR values match the applied input stimulus
* A new preload pattern is shifted in and retained in the BSR for a
subsequent EXTEST
* Update-DR under SAMPLE/PRELOAD does not drive pins (functional outputs
unchanged)
* `bsr++_++cg` (SAMPLE/PRELOAD operation) and `bsr++_++capture++_++cg`
(captured vs expected)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Apply known values to the boundary-scan input pins via the OCAH-local
BSR model.
. Load SAMPLE/PRELOAD (IR=0x03) through `OcahJtagTap` and perform
Capture-DR.
. Shift out captured data and compare against the applied stimulus in
the BSR reference model.
. Shift in a new preload pattern and perform Update-DR.
. Confirm functional outputs remain unchanged (SAMPLE/PRELOAD does not
drive pins) and the preload pattern is retained for later EXTEST use.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sample++_++capture++_++chk` |Captured BSR values match the applied
input stimulus

|`preload++_++no++_++drive++_++chk` |Functional outputs are unchanged
after Update-DR (no pin drive)

|`preload++_++retain++_++chk` |Preloaded pattern is retained in the BSR
for subsequent EXTEST
|===

*Pass Criteria*: SAMPLE captures live pin state matching stimulus while
PRELOAD leaves outputs undisturbed and retains the loaded pattern, with
zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

during CLAMP entry/exit transitions
|===

*Pass Criteria*: CLAMP holds the boundary outputs at their last driven

TDO = inverted TDI delayed by 1 bit.

delay.

delay.
. Confirm Capture-DR loaded the inverted default (1) and that no other
TDR is affected.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]

default (1) into the bypass register

|`inv++_++bypass++_++no++_++side++_++effect++_++chk` |No other TDRs are
disturbed during inverted bypass
|===

===== 0.13 `dtp++_++jtag++_++trst++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++trst++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`

*Objective*: TRST asynchronous TAP reset

*Test Coverage*:

* Asynchronous TRST++_++N forces the TAP to Test-Logic-Reset from any
state, independent of TCK
* `jtag++_++ptap++_++state++_++o` transitions to TLR within one TCK edge
of TRST++_++N assertion
* IR resets to IDCODE on TRST and scan state is cleared
* Normal operation resumes cleanly after TRST++_++N deassertion
(Run-Test/Idle, IDCODE readable)
* `trst++_++cg`: TRST asserted from different TAP states
* Power-on reset path: `pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni` forces the TAP to TLR
independent of TRST, confirming
`jtag++_++trst++_++n = pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni AND trst++_++n` (either
source resets the TAP)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive the TAP into a mid-operation non-reset state (e.g. Shift-DR) via
`OcahJtagTap`.
. Assert TRST++_++N asynchronously (independent of TCK).
. Confirm `jtag++_++ptap++_++state++_++o` transitions to
Test-Logic-Reset within one TCK edge and scan state is cleared.
. Release TRST++_++N and verify the IR contains IDCODE.
. Perform a normal DR scan to confirm IDCODE is readable and clean
recovery to Run-Test/Idle.
. With TRST++_++N deasserted, drive the TAP into a mid-operation state
and assert `pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni`; confirm the TAP enters TLR with
IR reset to IDCODE (POR path, independent of TRST).
. Release `pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni` and verify clean recovery,
confirming
`jtag++_++trst++_++n = pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni AND trst++_++n`.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`trst++_++to++_++tlr++_++chk` |TAP immediately enters TLR on TRST++_++N
assertion regardless of starting state

|`trst++_++ir++_++idcode++_++chk` |IR resets to IDCODE after TRST and
IDCODE is readable

|`trst++_++recovery++_++chk` |Normal operation resumes after TRST++_++N
deassertion

|`por++_++to++_++tlr++_++chk` |`pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni` forces TLR
(IR=IDCODE) independent of TRST, per
`jtag++_++trst++_++n = pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni AND trst++_++n`
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Asserting TRST++_++N or `pwr++_++on++_++rst++_++ni`
from any state drives the TAP to TLR with IR reset to IDCODE and clean
recovery afterward, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 1.1 `dtp++_++jtag++_++tmp++_++status++_++register++_++smoke++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++tmp++_++status++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`

*Test Coverage*:

* TMP++_++STATUS (IR=0x0C) 2-bit TDR selectable and shiftable on the
primary TAP
* TMP++_++STATUS++[++1++]++ reads 0 out of reset (no persistence active)
* TMP++_++STATUS accurately reflects the TMP FSM state when
CLAMP++_++HOLD engages Persistence-On
* TMP++_++STATUS reads are repeated with multiple caller-selected DR
shift values to expose unintended read side effects
* TMP++_++STATUS read causes no side effects on other TDRs or scan
routing

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Reset the primary TAP and read TMP++_++STATUS (IR=0x0C).
. Verify TMP++_++STATUS++[++1++]++ = 0 (no persistence active out of
reset).
. Re-read TMP++_++STATUS with 2-bit shift-in values `0b00`, `0b01`,
`0b10`, and `0b11`; verify persistence remains 0.
. Engage CLAMP++_++HOLD to drive the TMP FSM into Persistence-On.
. Re-select TMP++_++STATUS and verify TMP++_++STATUS++[++1++]++ = 1.
. Read IDCODE afterward to confirm TMP++_++STATUS access leaves adjacent
TDR routing intact.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description

out of reset

|`tmp++_++status++_++shift++_++value++_++chk` |Multiple TMP++_++STATUS
read shift values do not disturb persistence

|`tmp++_++status++_++fsm++_++track++_++chk` |TMP++_++STATUS++[++1++]++
tracks the TMP FSM Persistence-On state

|`tmp++_++status++_++side++_++effect++_++chk` |No unintended TDR or
scan-routing side effects from the read
|===

*Pass Criteria*: TMP++_++STATUS reads its reset value and tracks the TMP
FSM state across the persistence transition, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

===== 1.4 `dtp++_++jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`

*Objective*: IC++_++RESET override control

*Test Coverage*:

* IC++_++RESET (IR=0x0D) TDR independently overrides each chiplet reset
domain across three slices — SMC (nearest TDI), SEP, and External
(nearest TDO) — each gated by its
`IC++_++RESET++_{++SMC,SEP,EXT}++_++ENABLE` parameter
* Active-low `reset++_++enable` per IEEE 1149.1 §17:
`reset++_++enable=1` (default) disables the override (upstream reset
passes through), `reset++_++enable=0` enables it; the PTAP exports
`jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++ovrd++_++o++[++n++]++ = !reset++_++enable++[++n++]++`
and
`jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++ctrl++_++n++_++o++[++n++]++ = reset++_++control++[++n++]++`
* RESET++_++HOLD = 0 protects the IC++_++RESET register across TLR in
the RTL encoding
* RESET++_++HOLD = 1 allows TLR to restore the default all-ones value;
TRST always restores defaults
* Seeded random reset++_++enable/reset++_++control patterns exercise
reset++_++hold=0 persistence

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load IC++_++RESET (IR=0x0D) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Verify the default all-ones IC++_++RESET value and deasserted
flattened SMC/SEP/EXT outputs.
. For each SMC, SEP, and External slice, shift in RESET++_++ENABLE=0
(enable override) and RESET++_++CONTROL=0 (drive reset low); Update-DR.
[loweralpha]
.. Verify `jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++ovrd++_++o++[++0++]++` = 1 (=
`!reset++_++enable++[++0++]++`) and
`jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++ctrl++_++n++_++o++[++0++]++` = 0.
.. Set RESET++_++ENABLE++[++0++]++=1 (disable override); verify
`jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++ovrd++_++o++[++0++]++` = 0 (upstream reset
passes through), confirming the active-low
`ovrd++_++o = !reset++_++enable` mapping.
. Set RESET++_++HOLD=0 with a known RESET++_++ENABLE / RESET++_++CONTROL
pattern; Update-DR.
. Force Test-Logic-Reset (TLR); re-read IC++_++RESET.
[loweralpha]
.. Verify the programmed pattern is preserved.
. Repeat reset++_++hold=0 TLR preservation with seeded random
enable/control patterns.
. Set RESET++_++HOLD=1 with a non-default pattern; force TLR and verify
IC++_++RESET returns to its default all-ones value and override outputs
deassert.
. Program a non-default reset++_++hold=0 pattern, assert TRST, and
verify IC++_++RESET returns to defaults.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ic++_++reset++_++polarity++_++chk`
|`jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++ovrd++_++o++[++n++]++` ==
`!reset++_++enable++[++n++]++` (active-low enable per 1149.1 §17);
`jtag++_++ic++_++reset++_++ctrl++_++n++_++o++[++n++]++` ==
`reset++_++control++[++n++]++`

|`ic++_++reset++_++slice++_++chk` |Each slice (SMC/SEP/EXT) overrides
independently and is gated by its own
`IC++_++RESET++_{++SMC,SEP,EXT}++_++ENABLE`

|`ic++_++reset++_++hold++_++chk` |RESET++_++HOLD=0 preserves register
across TLR; RESET++_++HOLD=1 lets TLR restore defaults; TRST restores
defaults

|`ic++_++reset++_++random++_++hold++_++chk` |Seeded random
reset++_++hold=0 enable/control patterns preserve across TLR
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Each slice (SMC/SEP/EXT) is independently overridden
with the active-low `reset++_++enable` correctly mapped to
`ovrd++_++o = !reset++_++enable`, and RESET++_++HOLD correctly controls
TLR preservation/default restore behavior, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

`++[++59:0++]++`) reports compile-time parameter values
* Full-layout per-field checks: NUM++_++XTRIG++_++INT++_++CT
`++[++59:54++]++`, NUM++_++XTRIG++_++CTP `++[++53:48++]++`,
NUM++_++XTRA++_++STAP `++[++47:44++]++`,
STAP++_++IO++_++EN/SEP++_++DBG++_++EN/SMC++_++DBG++_++EN
`++[++43:41++]++`, NUM++_++SMC++_++IC++_++RST `++[++40:33++]++`,
NUM++_++SEP++_++IC++_++RST `++[++32:25++]++`, NUM++_++EXT++_++IC++_++RST
`++[++24:17++]++`, IC++_++RST++_++INST++_++EN `++[++16++]++`,
instruction-enable bits `++[++15:8++]++`, OCH++_++VER `++[++7:0++]++`
* Repeated reads return a stable value
* Register is read-only across all-zeros, all-ones, alternating, and
seeded-random write-attempt patterns
* CAPS read remains stable after switching through IDCODE and BYPASS
instructions

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load JTAG++_++CAPS (IR=0x19) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Capture-DR / Shift-DR the full 60-bit TDR (`++[++59:0++]++`).
. Compare each field against its compile-time parameter:
[loweralpha]
.. NUM++_++XTRIG++_++INT++_++CT == `XTRIG++_++NUM++_++INT++_++CT`;
NUM++_++XTRIG++_++CTP == `XTRIG++_++NUM++_++CTP`.
.. NUM++_++XTRA++_++STAP == `JTAG++_++NUM++_++EXTRA++_++STAPS`;
STAP++_++IO++_++EN/SEP++_++DBG++_++EN/SMC++_++DBG++_++EN == their enable
parameters.
.. NUM++_++SMC++_++IC++_++RST / NUM++_++SEP++_++IC++_++RST /
NUM++_++EXT++_++IC++_++RST == their per-slice IC++_++RESET width
parameters.
.. IC++_++RST++_++INST++_++EN and all instruction-enable bits
`++[++15:8++]++` == their parameters; OCH++_++VER ==
`JTAG++_++OCH++_++VER`.
. Read the register multiple times and verify all reads match.
. Attempt multiple write patterns (Shift-DR new data {plus} Update-DR),
then re-read after each attempt.
[loweralpha]
.. Verify the CAPS value is unchanged (read-only).
. Switch through IDCODE and BYPASS, then re-read JTAG++_++CAPS and
verify it is unchanged.

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]

`++[++59:0++]++` matches its compile-time parameter

|`jtag++_++caps++_++multi++_++read++_++chk` |Repeated reads are stable

|`jtag++_++caps++_++ro++_++chk` |Multiple write-attempt patterns leave
CAPS value unchanged

|`jtag++_++caps++_++side++_++effect++_++chk` |CAPS read remains stable
after instruction switches

verify CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP = 0.

|`cla++_++clock++_++stop++_++req++_++chk` |`stop++_++clks++_++o` follows

===== 1.8 `dtp++_++dbg++_++ctrl++_++clk++_++stop++_++random++_++clock++_++stop++_++test`

JTAG++_++CLOCK++_++STOP / CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP++_++EN combinations
crossed with `xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i`
* `stop++_++clks++_++o` = registered(`jtag++_++clock++_++stop` ++|++
`++|++xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i`) across all legal
combinations
* `cla++_++clock++_++stop++_++en++_++o` tracks
CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP++_++EN independently and never gates
`stop++_++clks++_++o`
* CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP readback reflects CLA requests only across the
randomized stimulus
* Per-source aggregation: each of the
up-to-`NUM++_++CLK++_++STOP++_++REQ` (default 9)
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i++[++i++]++` inputs individually
drives the OR-reduction into
`cla++_++clock++_++stop`/`stop++_++clks++_++o`, plus combinations of
multiple simultaneous request lines

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Generate legal combinations of JTAG++_++CLOCK++_++STOP,
CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP++_++EN, and
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i` vectors, including a directed
pass that walks a single `1` across each
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i++[++i++]++`
(`0..NUM++_++CLK++_++STOP++_++REQ-1`), multi-request masks, and seeded
random request/control combinations.
. For each combination, write DEBUG++_++CONTROL (IR=0x18) via
`OcahJtagTap` and drive the corresponding
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i`; Update-DR.
. Sample `stop++_++clks++_++o`, `cla++_++clock++_++stop++_++en++_++o`,
and the DEBUG++_++CONTROL readback fields.
. Compare against the bit-accurate reference model:
[loweralpha]
.. `stop++_++clks++_++o` == registered(`jtag++_++clock++_++stop` ++|++
OR of `xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i`).
.. `cla++_++clock++_++stop++_++en++_++o` ==
CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP++_++EN (no gating of `stop++_++clks++_++o`).
.. CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP readback == OR of
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i` only.

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description

matches registered(`jtag++_++clock++_++stop` OR reduction of
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i`) per combination

|`clk++_++stop++_++random++_++en++_++export++_++chk`
|`cla++_++clock++_++stop++_++en++_++o` tracks the TDR bit and never
gates `stop++_++clks++_++o`

|`clk++_++stop++_++random++_++readback++_++chk` |CLA++_++CLOCK++_++STOP
readback reflects CLA requests only

|`clk++_++stop++_++per++_++source++_++chk` |Each individual
`xtrig++_++clk++_++stop++_++req++_++i++[++i++]++` and multi-line
combinations OR-reduce into
`cla++_++clock++_++stop`/`stop++_++clks++_++o`
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Across all randomized and per-source combinations the

reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

===== 1.9 `dtp++_++dbg++_++ctrl++_++boot++_++stall++_++test`

and verify both flattened outputs plus DEBUG++_++CONTROL readback.

through multiple values to confirm the data field changes independently.

interaction combinations while BOOT++_++STALL++_++OVRD/BOOT++_++STALL

matches each programmed combination

===== 1.10 `dtp++_++dbg++_++smc++_++jtag2axi++_++caps++_++test`

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC++_++JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS (IR=0x27) 14-bit read-only TDR for the SMC
fabric AXI4 bridge
* Field decode: BUS++_++TYPE=0 (AXI4), ADDR++_++SIZE=56,
DATA++_++SIZE=64, RD++_++PL++_++DEPTH, WR++_++PL++_++DEPTH match
`SMC++_*++` parameters
* Repeated reads return a stable value
* Register is read-only across directed and seeded-random write-attempt
patterns
* CAPS read remains stable after switching through IDCODE and BYPASS
instructions

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SMC++_++JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS (IR=0x27) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Capture-DR / Shift-DR the 14-bit CAPS TDR.
. Compare each field against the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge parameters:
[loweralpha]
.. BUS++_++TYPE == 0 (AXI4); ADDR++_++SIZE == 56; DATA++_++SIZE == 64.
.. RD++_++PL++_++DEPTH and WR++_++PL++_++DEPTH == their `SMC++_*++`
pipeline-depth parameters.
. Read the register multiple times and verify all reads match.
. Attempt directed and seeded-random write patterns, then re-read after
each attempt; verify the CAPS value is unchanged.
. Switch through IDCODE and BYPASS, then re-read
SMC++_++JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS and verify it is unchanged.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++jtag2axi++_++caps++_++fields++_++chk`
|BUS++_++TYPE/ADDR++_++SIZE/DATA++_++SIZE/pipeline depths match
`SMC++_*++` parameters

|`smc++_++jtag2axi++_++caps++_++multi++_++read++_++chk` |Repeated reads
are stable

|`smc++_++jtag2axi++_++caps++_++ro++_++chk` |Multiple write-attempt
patterns leave CAPS value unchanged

|`smc++_++jtag2axi++_++caps++_++side++_++effect++_++chk` |CAPS read
remains stable after instruction switches
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All SMC++_++JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS fields match the SMC
fabric AXI4 bridge parameters and the register rejects writes, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 2.1 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++single++_++write++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric single write

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op AXI4 write through the SMC fabric debug bridge
(SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP, IR=0x28)
* 56-bit address / 64-bit data field formatting consistent with the SMC
fabric CAPS TDR (IR=0x27, ADDR++_++SIZE=56, DATA++_++SIZE=64)
* Address/data/wstrb delivery to the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate on
`axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++req++_++o` /
`axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++resp++_++i`
* Op-status transitions through Busy (0b11) and settles at OKAY (0b00)
after the write completes

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load and verify the bridge CAPS:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS (IR=0x27) via `OcahJtagTap` and read
the 14-bit CAPS TDR.
.. Verify bus++_++type = AXI4, ADDR++_++SIZE = 56, DATA++_++SIZE = 64.
. Issue the single write:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28).
.. Shift in the 56-bit target address, 64-bit write data, wstrb =
all-ones, size, and op = 0b10 (Write); then Update-DR.
. Poll for completion:
[loweralpha]
.. Re-enter Capture-DR/Shift-DR and read the op-status field.
.. Repeat until status is no longer Busy (0b11).
.. Verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).
. Verify the side effect:
[loweralpha]
.. Confirm the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate received exactly one AW/W beat
at the target address with the expected data and wstrb.
.. Confirm the captured B-response is OKAY.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++caps++_++chk` |CAPS TDR reports
bus++_++type=AXI4, ADDR++_++SIZE=56, DATA++_++SIZE=64

|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++status++_++chk` |Op-status passes through Busy
then returns OKAY (0b00)

|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++data++_++chk` |`OcahAxiRam` subordinate holds
the written address/data/wstrb
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The AXI4 single write completes with OKAY status and
the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate captures the correct address, data, and
wstrb, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 2.1a `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++single++_++write++_++data++_++verify++_++test`

===== 2.2 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++incr++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric incrementing series write

*Test Coverage*:

* A series of JTAG2AXI AXI4 writes through the SMC fabric bridge with
monotonically incrementing addresses (base, base{plus}stride, …)
* Per-beat op-status reaching OKAY (0b00) before the next address is
issued
* Distinct data pattern per address landing at the correct `OcahAxiRam`
location (no address aliasing)
* Address advance consistent with the 56-bit address / 64-bit data
geometry

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish the series parameters:
[loweralpha]
.. Choose a base address, a stride matching the 64-bit data width, and a
beat count N.
.. Build a per-beat data pattern (e.g. address-keyed) and mirror each
expected write in a reference memory.
. Issue the incrementing write series:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28).
.. For each beat i in 0..N-1: shift in address = base {plus}
i++*++stride, the i-th data word, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write);
Update-DR.
.. After each Update-DR, poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11)
and verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).
. Verify the resulting memory image:
[loweralpha]
.. For each address in the series, confirm the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate
holds the expected data and wstrb.
.. Confirm no writes landed outside the addressed range.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++status++_++chk` |Every beat returns
OKAY (0b00) after Busy

|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++addr++_++chk` |Captured AW addresses
follow base {plus} i++*++stride with no aliasing

|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++data++_++chk` |Each address holds its
distinct expected data pattern
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All N incrementing writes complete with OKAY status and
each target address holds its expected data, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

data word, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write); Update-DR.

and verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).

|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++no++_++incr++_++addr++_++chk` |All captured AW

===== 2.4 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++incr++_++with++_++error++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric series write with status

*Test Coverage*:

* An incrementing write series in which selected beats target an
error-generating region of the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate
* The non-OKAY op-status path (0b01 SLVERR / 0b10 DECERR) surfacing in
the op-status TDR for the erroring beats
* OKAY beats and erroring beats interleaved, confirming the bridge does
not hang or corrupt state after an error
* Recovery: a subsequent legal write completes with OKAY after an
erroring beat

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the error stimulus:
[loweralpha]
.. Program the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate (or its AXI wrapper) to return a
non-OKAY response for a designated error region.
.. Choose an incrementing series in which some beats fall inside the
error region and some are legal.
. Issue the series and capture per-beat status:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28).
.. For each beat: shift in address = base {plus} i++*++stride, data,
wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
.. Poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11) and record the
op-status field.
.. For legal beats verify op = 0b00 (OKAY); for error beats verify op
reflects the error code (0b01 SLVERR or 0b10 DECERR).
. Confirm containment and recovery:
[loweralpha]
.. Issue a final legal write to a clean address and verify op = 0b00
(OKAY).
.. Verify legal-beat data landed correctly and no spurious writes
resulted from the erroring beats.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++status++_++chk` |Error beats report
SLVERR/DECERR; legal beats report OKAY

|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++data++_++chk` |Only legal-beat
addresses are updated in the subordinate

|`smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++recovery++_++chk` |A post-error legal
write completes with OKAY (no hang/corruption)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Erroring beats report the expected non-OKAY status,
legal beats and the post-error write complete with OKAY, and memory
matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model
errors.

'''''

===== 2.5 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++random++_++ops++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric randomized write operations

*Test Coverage*:

* Constrained-random JTAG2AXI AXI4 writes through the SMC fabric bridge
over randomized address, data, wstrb, and size
* Stress of the 56-bit address / 64-bit data field packing across the
SINGLE++_++OP TDR
* A reference memory mirroring every issued write, compared against the
`OcahAxiRam` subordinate at the end
* Op-status consistency (OKAY for in-range writes) across a long
randomized sequence

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Generate the randomized workload:
[loweralpha]
.. Build a constrained-random set of write operations (random in-range
address, random data, random legal wstrb, random size) for the SMC
fabric AXI4 bridge.
.. Apply each accepted write to a reference memory model.
. Issue each operation:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28).
.. For each randomized op: shift in address, data, wstrb, size, op =
0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
.. Poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11) and verify op = 0b00
(OKAY).
. Compare end state:
[loweralpha]
.. Read back / inspect the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate and compare every
written location against the reference memory.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++axi++_++rand++_++status++_++chk` |Every randomized write
returns OKAY (0b00)

|`smc++_++axi++_++rand++_++mem++_++chk` |Final subordinate image matches
the reference memory exactly
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All randomized writes complete with OKAY status and the
final subordinate memory image matches the reference model, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 2.6 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++write++_++security++_++gating++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric write security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc++_++jtag2axi++_++security++_++disable = !feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug ++||++ !feat++_++ctrl++_++i.ap++_++debug`
— either required enable low disables the SMC fabric bridge
* When gated, SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28) falls back to
BYPASS (1-bit DR) and no AXI traffic appears on
`axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++req++_++o`
* Independent gating by driving each of `ap++_++debug` and
`soc++_++debug` low, then full restoration after restoring enables high
* A legal write succeeds before gating and again after restoration
(clean re-enable)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Baseline write (un-gated):
[loweralpha]
.. With both required enables high, issue one
SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP write (IR=0x28, op=0b10) and verify op =
0b00 (OKAY) and the `OcahAxiRam` subordinate captured it.
. Gate via `ap++_++debug`:
[loweralpha]
.. Drive required enable `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.ap++_++debug` = 0.
.. Load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28) and attempt a write;
verify the instruction selects BYPASS (1-bit DR).
.. Verify no AW/W transaction is issued on
`axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++req++_++o`.
.. Restore `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.ap++_++debug` = 1 and verify a legal
write again completes with OKAY.
. Gate via `soc++_++debug`:
[loweralpha]
.. Drive required enable `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug` = 0 and
repeat the BYPASS / no-AXI-traffic check.
.. Restore `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug` = 1 and verify normal
operation restored.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++axi++_++gate++_++bypass++_++chk` |With either required enable
low, IR=0x28 selects BYPASS (1-bit DR)

|`smc++_++axi++_++gate++_++no++_++traffic++_++chk` |No AXI write appears
on `axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++req++_++o` while gated

|`smc++_++axi++_++gate++_++restore++_++chk` |A legal write completes
with OKAY after each required enable is restored
|===

*Pass Criteria*: With `soc++_++debug` or `ap++_++debug` driven low the
write is gated to BYPASS with no AXI traffic, and restoring each enable
high restores a passing OKAY write, with zero scoreboard/reference-model
errors.

'''''

===== 2.7 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++single++_++write++_++test`

*Source Module*:
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP single write

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op AXI4-Lite write through the SMC OTP bridge
(SMC++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP, IR=0x1C)
* 32-bit address / 32-bit data field formatting consistent with the SMC
OTP CAPS TDR (IR=0x1B, AXI4-Lite, 32/32)
* Address/data/wstrb delivery to the AXI-Lite subordinate on
`axil++_++smc++_++otp++_++jtag++_++req++_++o`
* Op-status transitions through Busy (0b11) and settles at OKAY (0b00)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load and verify the bridge CAPS:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++OTP++_++JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS (IR=0x1B) via `OcahJtagTap`
and read the 14-bit CAPS TDR.
.. Verify bus++_++type = AXI4-Lite, ADDR++_++SIZE = 32, DATA++_++SIZE =
32.
. Issue the single write:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x1C).
.. Shift in the 32-bit target address, 32-bit write data, wstrb =
all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
. Poll for completion:
[loweralpha]
.. Re-enter Capture-DR/Shift-DR and read the op-status field until not
Busy (0b11).
.. Verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).
. Verify the side effect:
[loweralpha]
.. Confirm the AXI-Lite subordinate received the write at the target
address with the expected data and wstrb.
.. Confirm the captured B-response is OKAY.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++caps++_++chk` |CAPS TDR reports
bus++_++type=AXI4-Lite, 32-bit address/data

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++status++_++chk` |Op-status passes
through Busy then returns OKAY (0b00)

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++data++_++chk` |AXI-Lite subordinate
holds the written address/data/wstrb
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The AXI4-Lite single write completes with OKAY status
and the SMC OTP subordinate captures the correct address and data, with
zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 2.7a `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++single++_++write++_++data++_++verify++_++test`

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]

equals the written SMC OTP word

|`smc++_++otp++_++wr++_++status++_++okay++_++chk` |Write and read
statuses both settle to OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The SMC OTP written word is returned by bridge
read-back with OKAY status and zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

===== 2.8 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++incr++_++test`

*Source Module*:
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP incrementing series write

*Test Coverage*:

* A series of JTAG2AXI AXI4-Lite writes through the SMC OTP bridge with
incrementing 32-bit addresses (base, base{plus}stride, …)
* Per-beat op-status reaching OKAY (0b00) before advancing the address
* Distinct data pattern per address landing at the correct AXI-Lite
subordinate location (no aliasing)
* Address advance consistent with the 32-bit data geometry

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish the series parameters:
[loweralpha]
.. Choose a base address, a 32-bit-word stride, and a beat count N.
.. Build a per-beat data pattern and mirror each expected write in a
reference memory.
. Issue the incrementing write series:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x1C).
.. For each beat i in 0..N-1: shift in address = base {plus}
i++*++stride, the i-th data word, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write);
Update-DR.
.. After each Update-DR, poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11)
and verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).
. Verify the resulting memory image:
[loweralpha]
.. For each address in the series, confirm the AXI-Lite subordinate
holds the expected data.
.. Confirm no writes landed outside the addressed range.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++status++_++chk` |Every beat
returns OKAY (0b00) after Busy

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++addr++_++chk` |Captured AW
addresses follow base {plus} i++*++stride with no aliasing

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++data++_++chk` |Each address
holds its distinct expected data pattern
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All N incrementing writes complete with OKAY status and
each target address holds its expected data, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

write-over-write at one AXI-Lite subordinate location

* Final memory content equals the last data beat written

data word, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
.. After each Update-DR, poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11)
and verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).
. Verify the single location:
[loweralpha]
.. Confirm all N writes targeted exactly the one fixed address on
`axil++_++smc++_++otp++_++jtag++_++req++_++o`.
.. Confirm the AXI-Lite subordinate holds the last beat’s data at that
address.

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++no++_++incr++_++status++_++chk` |Each
repeated write returns OKAY (0b00)

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++no++_++incr++_++addr++_++chk` |All
captured AW addresses equal the single fixed address

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++no++_++incr++_++data++_++chk` |Fixed
location holds the final (last-beat) data
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All N fixed-address writes complete with OKAY status
and the single target location holds the last data beat, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

===== 2.10 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++incr++_++with++_++error++_++test`

*Source Module*:
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP series write with status

*Test Coverage*:

* An incrementing AXI4-Lite write series in which selected beats target
an error-generating region of the SMC OTP subordinate
* The non-OKAY op-status path (0b01 SLVERR / 0b10 DECERR) surfacing in
the op-status TDR for erroring beats
* OKAY and erroring beats interleaved, confirming the bridge does not
hang or corrupt state after an error
* Recovery: a subsequent legal write completes with OKAY after an
erroring beat

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the error stimulus:
[loweralpha]
.. Program the AXI-Lite subordinate (or its wrapper) to return a
non-OKAY response for a designated error region.
.. Choose an incrementing series in which some beats fall inside the
error region and some are legal.
. Issue the series and capture per-beat status:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x1C).
.. For each beat: shift in address = base {plus} i++*++stride, data,
wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
.. Poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11) and record the
op-status field.
.. For legal beats verify op = 0b00 (OKAY); for error beats verify op
reflects the error code (0b01 SLVERR or 0b10 DECERR).
. Confirm containment and recovery:
[loweralpha]
.. Issue a final legal write to a clean address and verify op = 0b00
(OKAY).
.. Verify legal-beat data landed correctly and no spurious writes
resulted from the erroring beats.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++status++_++chk` |Error beats
report SLVERR/DECERR; legal beats report OKAY

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++data++_++chk` |Only legal-beat
addresses are updated in the subordinate

|`smc++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++recovery++_++chk` |A post-error
legal write completes with OKAY (no hang/corruption)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Erroring beats report the expected non-OKAY status,
legal beats and the post-error write complete with OKAY, and memory
matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model
errors.

'''''

===== 2.14 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++incr++_++test`

*Source Module*:
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP incrementing series write

*Test Coverage*:

* A series of JTAG2AXI AXI4-Lite writes through the SEP OTP bridge with
incrementing 32-bit addresses (base, base{plus}stride, …)
* Per-beat op-status reaching OKAY (0b00) before advancing the address
* Distinct data pattern per address landing at the correct AXI-Lite
subordinate location (no aliasing)
* Address advance consistent with the 32-bit data geometry

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish the series parameters:
[loweralpha]
.. Choose a base address, a 32-bit-word stride, and a beat count N.
.. Build a per-beat data pattern and mirror each expected write in a
reference memory.
. Issue the incrementing write series:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SEP++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x22).
.. For each beat i in 0..N-1: shift in address = base {plus}
i++*++stride, the i-th data word, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write);
Update-DR.
.. After each Update-DR, poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11)
and verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).
. Verify the resulting memory image:
[loweralpha]
.. For each address in the series, confirm the AXI-Lite subordinate
holds the expected data.
.. Confirm no writes landed outside the addressed range.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++status++_++chk` |Every beat
returns OKAY (0b00) after Busy

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++addr++_++chk` |Captured AW
addresses follow base {plus} i++*++stride with no aliasing

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++incr++_++data++_++chk` |Each address
holds its distinct expected data pattern
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All N incrementing writes complete with OKAY status and
each target address holds its expected data, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 2.15 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++no++_++incr++_++test`

*Source Module*:
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

targeting one fixed 32-bit address (no-incr mode)
* Each beat re-uses the same address with fresh data, exercising
write-over-write at one AXI-Lite subordinate location

* Final memory content equals the last data beat written

[arabic]
. Establish the fixed-address series:
[loweralpha]

words.

reference memory.

[loweralpha]
.. Load SEP++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x22).
.. For each beat i in 0..N-1: shift in the same target address, the i-th
data word, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
.. After each Update-DR, poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11)
and verify op = 0b00 (OKAY).
. Verify the single location:
[loweralpha]
.. Confirm all N writes targeted exactly the one fixed address on
`axil++_++sep++_++otp++_++jtag++_++req++_++o`.
.. Confirm the AXI-Lite subordinate holds the last beat’s data at that
address.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++no++_++incr++_++status++_++chk` |Each
repeated write returns OKAY (0b00)

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++no++_++incr++_++addr++_++chk` |All
captured AW addresses equal the single fixed address

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++no++_++incr++_++data++_++chk` |Fixed
location holds the final (last-beat) data
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All N fixed-address writes complete with OKAY status
and the single target location holds the last data beat, with zero
scoreboard/reference-model errors.

===== 2.16 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++incr++_++with++_++error++_++test`

*Source Module*:
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP series write with status

*Test Coverage*:

* An incrementing AXI4-Lite write series in which selected beats target
an error-generating region of the SEP OTP subordinate
* The non-OKAY op-status path (0b01 SLVERR / 0b10 DECERR) surfacing in
the op-status TDR for erroring beats
* OKAY and erroring beats interleaved, confirming the bridge does not
hang or corrupt state after an error
* Recovery: a subsequent legal write completes with OKAY after an
erroring beat

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the error stimulus:
[loweralpha]
.. Program the AXI-Lite subordinate (or its wrapper) to return a
non-OKAY response for a designated error region.
.. Choose an incrementing series in which some beats fall inside the
error region and some are legal.
. Issue the series and capture per-beat status:
[loweralpha]
.. Load SEP++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x22).
.. For each beat: shift in address = base {plus} i++*++stride, data,
wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
.. Poll Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (0b11) and record the
op-status field.
.. For legal beats verify op = 0b00 (OKAY); for error beats verify op
reflects the error code (0b01 SLVERR or 0b10 DECERR).
. Confirm containment and recovery:
[loweralpha]
.. Issue a final legal write to a clean address and verify op = 0b00
(OKAY).
.. Verify legal-beat data landed correctly and no spurious writes
resulted from the erroring beats.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++status++_++chk` |Error beats
report SLVERR/DECERR; legal beats report OKAY

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++data++_++chk` |Only legal-beat
addresses are updated in the subordinate

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++err++_++recovery++_++chk` |A post-error
legal write completes with OKAY (no hang/corruption)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Erroring beats report the expected non-OKAY status,
legal beats and the post-error write complete with OKAY, and memory
matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model
errors.

'''''

===== 2.18 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++write++_++security++_++gating++_++test`

*Source Module*:
`dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++wr++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP write security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* `sep++_++otp++_++jtag2axi++_++security++_++disable = !feat++_++ctrl++_++i.fuse++_++test ++||++ !feat++_++ctrl++_++i.sep++_++debug ++||++ !feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug ++||++ !feat++_++ctrl++_++i.ap++_++debug`
— any required enable low disables the SEP OTP bridge
* When gated, SEP++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x22) falls back
to BYPASS (1-bit DR) and no AXI-Lite traffic appears on
`axil++_++sep++_++otp++_++jtag++_++req++_++o`
* Independent gating by driving each of `fuse++_++test`,
`sep++_++debug`, `soc++_++debug`, and `ap++_++debug` low, then full
restoration after restoring enables high
* A legal write succeeds before gating and again after restoration
(clean re-enable)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Baseline write (un-gated):
[loweralpha]
.. With all four required enables high, issue one
SEP++_++OTP++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP write (IR=0x22, op=0b10) and
verify op = 0b00 (OKAY) and the AXI-Lite subordinate captured it.
. Gate via each control, one at a time:
[loweralpha]
.. Drive required enable `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.ap++_++debug` = 0; load
IR=0x22 and attempt a write; verify the instruction selects BYPASS
(1-bit DR) and no transaction on
`axil++_++sep++_++otp++_++jtag++_++req++_++o`; restore and verify normal
operation.
.. Repeat for `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug` = 0.
.. Repeat for `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.sep++_++debug` = 0.
.. Repeat for `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.fuse++_++test` = 0.
. Final restore check:
[loweralpha]
.. With all required enables high, verify a legal write again completes
with OKAY.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++gate++_++bypass++_++chk` |With any required
enable low, IR=0x22 selects BYPASS (1-bit DR)

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++gate++_++no++_++traffic++_++chk` |No AXI-Lite
write appears on `axil++_++sep++_++otp++_++jtag++_++req++_++o` while
gated

|`sep++_++otp++_++axi++_++gate++_++restore++_++chk` |A legal write
completes with OKAY after each required enable is restored
|===

*Pass Criteria*: With `fuse++_++test`, `sep++_++debug`, `soc++_++debug`,
or `ap++_++debug` driven low the write is gated to BYPASS with no
AXI-Lite traffic, and restoring each enable high restores a passing OKAY
write, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

==== 3. JTAG2AXI Read Scenarios

===== 3.1 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++single++_++write++_++read++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++rd++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric write-read path

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC fabric AXI4 single-operation write followed by a read-back of the
same address
* SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28) DR field packing: address
(56-bit), data, wstrb, size, op
* Read op encoding `op=0b01` with data=0 and wstrb=0, and busy/terminal
status polling
* Data integrity between the written word and the value returned by
`OcahAxiRam`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SMC++_++JTAG2AXI++_++CAPS (IR=0x27) and read the 14-bit CAPS TDR.
[loweralpha]
.. Verify bus++_++type = AXI4, addr++_++size = 56, data++_++size = 64.
. Issue a single write through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge to a chosen
target address.
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28).
.. Shift in target address, write data, wstrb=all-ones, size, op=0b10
(Write); Update-DR.
.. Poll via Capture-DR/Shift-DR until op status leaves Busy (0b11);
verify op=0b00 (OKAY).
. Read back the same address through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge.
[loweralpha]
.. Reload SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28).
.. Shift in target address, data=0, wstrb=0, size, op=0b01 (Read);
Update-DR.
.. Poll op status until not Busy (0b11), then read returned data and
status from the DR.
. Compare the returned read word against the previously written value
and the `OcahAxiRam` reference.
. Verify the terminal op status is 0b00 (OKAY) on both the write and the
read.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++axi++_++single++_++wr++_++rd++_++status++_++chk` |Write and
read both reach op=0b00 (OKAY) after leaving Busy

|`smc++_++axi++_++single++_++wr++_++rd++_++rdata++_++chk` |Returned read
word equals the written value and the `OcahAxiRam` model

|`smc++_++axi++_++single++_++wr++_++rd++_++mem++_++chk` |`OcahAxiRam`
content at the target address matches the issued write
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The write completes OKAY, the read-back returns the
same word with OKAY status, and the memory side effect matches, with
zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

series reaches op=0b00 (OKAY) after Busy clears

matches the incrementing-address reference value

===== 3.4 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++series++_++write++_++read++_++incr++_++with++_++error++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++rd++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric write-read with error status

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC fabric AXI4 incrementing series that intersects an
error-generating subordinate region
* Error response capture in the op++[++1:0++]++ status (SLVERR 0b01 /
DECERR 0b10)
* Bridge does not hang, corrupt the DR, or lose state after a non-OKAY
response
* Recovery: a subsequent legal read to a good address completes OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure `OcahAxiRam` (or its error wrapper) so a subset of the
incrementing block returns SLVERR/DECERR.
. Issue the incrementing read series through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge.
[loweralpha]
.. For each address, load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28); shift
in address{plus}i++*++stride, data=0, wstrb=0, op=0b01 (Read);
Update-DR.
.. Poll until not Busy; capture returned data and status per op.
. For addresses in the error region, verify op reflects the expected
error code (0b01/0b10).
. For addresses in the good region, verify op=0b00 (OKAY) and correct
data.
. After the error ops, issue one more legal read to a good address and
confirm OKAY and correct data (recovery).

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++axi++_++incr++_++error++_++status++_++chk` |Error-region ops
report the expected SLVERR/DECERR op code

|`smc++_++axi++_++incr++_++error++_++rdata++_++chk` |Good-region beats
match the reference; error beats are not mistaken for valid data

|`smc++_++axi++_++incr++_++error++_++mem++_++chk` |`OcahAxiRam`
good-region content matches expectations

|`smc++_++axi++_++incr++_++error++_++robustness++_++chk` |Bridge
recovers and a post-error legal read completes OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Each op reaches its expected terminal status (OKAY or
the configured error), the bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent
legal read, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 3.5 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++read++_++random++_++ops++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++rd++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric randomized reads

*Test Coverage*:

* Constrained-random SMC fabric AXI4 read addresses and sizes against a
randomized reference
* SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28) field packing under randomized
address/size stimulus
* Per-op data integrity across a randomized address space
* Status correctness (OKAY) for all in-range randomized reads

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload `OcahAxiRam` with a randomized reference image over the SMC
fabric read window.
. Generate a constrained-random sequence of in-range read addresses (and
legal sizes).
. Issue each randomized read through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge.
[loweralpha]
.. Load SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28); shift in the random
address, data=0, wstrb=0, size, op=0b01 (Read); Update-DR.

status.

that address.

|Checker Type |Description

reaches op=0b00 (OKAY) after Busy clears

|`smc++_++axi++_++random++_++rdata++_++chk` |Each returned word matches
the randomized `OcahAxiRam` reference
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All randomized reads complete OKAY and every returned
word matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model
errors.

'''''

===== 3.6 `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++read++_++security++_++gating++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++rd++_++test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah++_++jtag++_++vip`, `ocah++_++axi++_++vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric read security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc++_++jtag2axi++_++security++_++disable = !soc++_++debug ++||++ !ap++_++debug`
independently gates the SMC fabric read bridge when a required enable is
low
* Gated JTAG2AXI instructions fall back to BYPASS (1-bit DR); no AXI
traffic on `axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++req++_++o`
* Normal read operation restored after the required enable is restored

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish a baseline SMC fabric read returning OKAY with expected data
from `OcahAxiRam`.
. Drive required enable `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.ap++_++debug=0`; load
SMC++_++AXI++_++SINGLE++_++OP (IR=0x28) and attempt a read.
[loweralpha]
.. Verify the instruction selects BYPASS (DR length 1 bit) and no AXI
transaction issues on `axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++req++_++o`.
. Restore `ap++_++debug=1`; verify normal read restored.
. Repeat steps 2-3 for `feat++_++ctrl++_++i.soc++_++debug=0`.

*Checkers*:

[width="100%",cols="52%,48%",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`smc++_++rd++_++gate++_++bypass++_++chk` |DR is 1-bit BYPASS while
`ap++_++debug` or `soc++_++debug` is low

|`smc++_++rd++_++gate++_++noaxi++_++chk` |No AXI transaction on
`axi++_++smc++_++dbg++_++req++_++o` while gated

|`smc++_++rd++_++gate++_++restore++_++chk` |Normal read with OKAY status
resumes after the bit clears
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Each required enable independently forces BYPASS with
no AXI traffic when low, and reads resume correctly once restored high,
with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 3.6a `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++read++_++security++_++gating++_++no++_++axi++_++activity++_++test`

*Source Module*: `dtp++_++jtag2axi++_++smc++_++axi++_++rd++_++test`

request from reaching AXI
